{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-fable-5-1","id":"msg_011Cg6R2TZMfLPa7BySmkuy8","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The nbody benchmark is now parallelized with OpenMP. Only `nbody/nbody.cpp` and `nbody/CMakeLists.txt` were changed, and the executable is still named `nbody`.\n\n**What changed**\n\n- **Force computation** is split across threads by body row with a static schedule. Each row reads all positions and writes only its own velocity, so rows are independent. The inner summation order is unchanged.\n- **Position integration** is a second worksharing loop. Both loops are orphaned `omp for` constructs inside one parallel region that spans the whole step loop, so there is a single fork per run and only implicit barriers between phases.\n- **Thread count is capped** at one thread per 16 bodies. On this 256-thread machine, running all threads on a small problem was slower than the serial code due to fork and barrier overhead.\n- **Energy validation** uses parallel reductions, with dynamic scheduling for the triangular potential-energy loop.\n- **CMake** now requires OpenMP unconditionally via `find_package(OpenMP REQUIRED)` and links `OpenMP::OpenMP_CXX`. No new dependencies beyond the compiler's own OpenMP runtime.\n\n**Correctness**\n\nThe final body states are bit-identical to the original serial binary. Getting there needed one extra step: the compiler fused the velocity update into an FMA once the loop was outlined for OpenMP, which changed the last bits and the result hash. I disabled implicit contraction with `-ffp-contract=off` and wrote the FMAs the original build used explicitly with `std::fma`, so evaluation no longer depends on how the compiler outlines the code. Verified identical hashes, sums, and samples for 8, 1000, 1024, and 4096 bodies and across 1, 3, 5, 7, and 256 threads. The only visible difference is the energy diagnostic, which can differ in the last printed digit because the parallel reduction changes the summation order. The serial build itself shifts by the same amount when contraction is toggled.\n\n**Performance** at 10 steps on a 2-socket EPYC 7763 (128 cores):\n\n| Bodies | Serial | OpenMP | Threads used |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| 1024 | 77 ms | 8 ms | 64 |\n| 4096 | 1236 ms | 120 ms | 256 |\n| 16384 | 19957 ms (1 thread) | 180 to 230 ms | 256 |\n\nScaling from 1 to 128 threads on 16384 bodies is near-linear at every doubling. Pinning threads with `OMP_PROC_BIND=close OMP_PLACES=cores` gives a further small gain on large sizes."}],"container":null,"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":32,"cache_creation_input_tokens":1405,"cache_read_input_tokens":43425,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":1405},"output_tokens":705,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"global","speed":"standard"},"input_transformations":[],"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"3ebd36c0-aaa4-49af-a8be-3c712f181dfe","uuid":"6514c91b-27e7-493a-b80f-439a9eda9ea5","timestamp":"2026-10-16T18:48:07.403Z","request_id":"req_011Cg6R2TGkCKnskLGmUzjxQ"}
